2306 enum class arg_id_kind { none, index, name }; 
2307  
2308 // An argument reference. 
2309 template <typename Char> struct arg_ref { 
2310   FMT_CONSTEXPR arg_ref() : kind(arg_id_kind::none), val() {} 
2311  
2312   FMT_CONSTEXPR explicit arg_ref(int index) 
2313       : kind(arg_id_kind::index), val(index) {} 
2314   FMT_CONSTEXPR explicit arg_ref(basic_string_view<Char> name) 
2315       : kind(arg_id_kind::name), val(name) {} 
2316  
2317   FMT_CONSTEXPR arg_ref& operator=(int idx) { 
2318     kind = arg_id_kind::index; 
2319     val.index = idx; 
2320     return *this; 
2321   } 
2322  
2323   arg_id_kind kind; 
2324   union value { 
2325     FMT_CONSTEXPR value(int id = 0) : index{id} {} 
2326     FMT_CONSTEXPR value(basic_string_view<Char> n) : name(n) {} 
2327  
2328     int index; 
2329     basic_string_view<Char> name; 
2330   } val; 
2331 };

2333 // Format specifiers with width and precision resolved at formatting rather 
2334 // than parsing time to allow re-using the same parsed specifiers with 
2335 // different sets of arguments (precompilation of format strings). 
2336 template <typename Char> 
2337 struct dynamic_format_specs : basic_format_specs<Char> { 
2338   arg_ref<Char> width_ref; 
2339   arg_ref<Char> precision_ref; 
2340 }; 
2341  
2342 // Format spec handler that saves references to arguments representing dynamic 
2343 // width and precision to be resolved at formatting time. 
2344 template <typename ParseContext> 
2345 class dynamic_specs_handler 
2346     : public specs_setter<typename ParseContext::char_type> { 
2347  public: 
2348   using char_type = typename ParseContext::char_type; 
2349  
2350   FMT_CONSTEXPR dynamic_specs_handler(dynamic_format_specs<char_type>& specs, 
2351                                       ParseContext& ctx) 
2352       : specs_setter<char_type>(specs), specs_(specs), context_(ctx) {} 
2353  
2354   FMT_CONSTEXPR dynamic_specs_handler(const dynamic_specs_handler& other) 
2355       : specs_setter<char_type>(other), 
2356         specs_(other.specs_), 
2357         context_(other.context_) {} 
2358  
2359   template <typename Id> FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_dynamic_width(Id arg_id) { 
2360     specs_.width_ref = make_arg_ref(arg_id); 
2361   } 
2362  
2363   template <typename Id> FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_dynamic_precision(Id arg_id) { 
2364     specs_.precision_ref = make_arg_ref(arg_id); 
2365   } 
2366  
2367   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_error(const char* message) { 
2368     context_.on_error(message); 
2369   } 
2370  
2371  private: 
2372   using arg_ref_type = arg_ref<char_type>; 
2373  
2374   FMT_CONSTEXPR arg_ref_type make_arg_ref(int arg_id) { 
2375     context_.check_arg_id(arg_id); 
2376     return arg_ref_type(arg_id); 
2377   } 
2378  
2379   FMT_CONSTEXPR arg_ref_type make_arg_ref(auto_id) { 
2380     return arg_ref_type(context_.next_arg_id()); 
2381   } 
2382  
2383   FMT_CONSTEXPR arg_ref_type make_arg_ref(basic_string_view<char_type> arg_id) { 
2384     context_.check_arg_id(arg_id); 
2385     basic_string_view<char_type> format_str( 
2386         context_.begin(), to_unsigned(context_.end() - context_.begin())); 
2387     return arg_ref_type(arg_id); 
2388   } 
2389  
2390   dynamic_format_specs<char_type>& specs_; 
2391   ParseContext& context_; 
2392 }; 
2393  
2394 template <typename Char, typename IDHandler> 
2395 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_arg_id(const Char* begin, const Char* end, 
2396                                        IDHandler&& handler) { 
2397   FMT_ASSERT(begin != end, ""); 
2398   Char c = *begin; 
2399   if (c == '}' || c == ':') { 
2400     handler(); 
2401     return begin; 
2402   } 
2403   if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') { 
2404     int index = 0; 
2405     if (c != '0') 
2406       index = parse_nonnegative_int(begin, end, handler); 
2407     else 
2408       ++begin; 
2409     if (begin == end || (*begin != '}' && *begin != ':')) 
2410       handler.on_error("invalid format string"); 
2411     else 
2412       handler(index); 
2413     return begin; 
2414   } 
2415   if (!is_name_start(c)) { 
2416     handler.on_error("invalid format string"); 
2417     return begin; 
2418   } 
2419   auto it = begin; 
2420   do { 
2421     ++it; 
2422   } while (it != end && (is_name_start(c = *it) || ('0' <= c && c <= '9'))); 
2423   handler(basic_string_view<Char>(begin, to_unsigned(it - begin))); 
2424   return it; 
2425 } 
2426  
2427 // Adapts SpecHandler to IDHandler API for dynamic width. 
2428 template <typename SpecHandler, typename Char> struct width_adapter { 
2429   explicit FMT_CONSTEXPR width_adapter(SpecHandler& h) : handler(h) {} 
2430  
2431   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()() { handler.on_dynamic_width(auto_id()); } 
2432   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(int id) { handler.on_dynamic_width(id); } 
2433   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(basic_string_view<Char> id) { 
2434     handler.on_dynamic_width(id); 
2435   } 
2436  
2437   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_error(const char* message) { 
2438     handler.on_error(message); 
2439   } 
2440  
2441   SpecHandler& handler; 
2442 }; 
2443  
2444 // Adapts SpecHandler to IDHandler API for dynamic precision. 
2445 template <typename SpecHandler, typename Char> struct precision_adapter { 
2446   explicit FMT_CONSTEXPR precision_adapter(SpecHandler& h) : handler(h) {} 
2447  
2448   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()() { handler.on_dynamic_precision(auto_id()); } 
2449   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(int id) { handler.on_dynamic_precision(id); } 
2450   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(basic_string_view<Char> id) { 
2451     handler.on_dynamic_precision(id); 
2452   } 
2453  
2454   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_error(const char* message) { 
2455     handler.on_error(message); 
2456   } 
2457  
2458   SpecHandler& handler; 
2459 }; 
2460  
2461 template <typename Char> 
2462 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* next_code_point(const Char* begin, const Char* end) { 
2463   if (const_check(sizeof(Char) != 1) || (*begin & 0x80) == 0) return begin + 1; 
2464   do { 
2465     ++begin; 
2466   } while (begin != end && (*begin & 0xc0) == 0x80); 
2467   return begin; 
2468 } 
2469  
2470 // Parses fill and alignment. 
2471 template <typename Char, typename Handler> 
2472 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_align(const Char* begin, const Char* end, 
2473                                       Handler&& handler) { 
2474   FMT_ASSERT(begin != end, ""); 
2475   auto align = align::none; 
2476   auto p = next_code_point(begin, end); 
2477   if (p == end) p = begin; 
2478   for (;;) { 
2479     switch (static_cast<char>(*p)) { 
2480     case '<': 
2481       align = align::left; 
2482       break; 
2483     case '>': 
2484       align = align::right; 
2485       break; 
2486 #if FMT_DEPRECATED_NUMERIC_ALIGN 
2487     case '=': 
2488       align = align::numeric; 
2489       break; 
2490 #endif 
2491     case '^': 
2492       align = align::center; 
2493       break; 
2494     } 
2495     if (align != align::none) { 
2496       if (p != begin) { 
2497         auto c = *begin; 
2498         if (c == '{') 
2499           return handler.on_error("invalid fill character '{'"), begin; 
2500         handler.on_fill(basic_string_view<Char>(begin, to_unsigned(p - begin))); 
2501         begin = p + 1; 
2502       } else 
2503         ++begin; 
2504       handler.on_align(align); 
2505       break; 
2506     } else if (p == begin) { 
2507       break; 
2508     } 
2509     p = begin; 
2510   } 
2511   return begin; 
2512 } 
2513  
2514 template <typename Char, typename Handler> 
2515 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_width(const Char* begin, const Char* end, 
2516                                       Handler&& handler) { 
2517   FMT_ASSERT(begin != end, ""); 
2518   if ('0' <= *begin && *begin <= '9') { 
2519     handler.on_width(parse_nonnegative_int(begin, end, handler)); 
2520   } else if (*begin == '{') { 
2521     ++begin; 
2522     if (begin != end) 
2523       begin = parse_arg_id(begin, end, width_adapter<Handler, Char>(handler)); 
2524     if (begin == end || *begin != '}') 
2525       return handler.on_error("invalid format string"), begin; 
2526     ++begin; 
2527   } 
2528   return begin; 
2529 } 
2530  
2531 template <typename Char, typename Handler> 
2532 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_precision(const Char* begin, const Char* end, 
2533                                           Handler&& handler) { 
2534   ++begin; 
2535   auto c = begin != end ? *begin : Char(); 
2536   if ('0' <= c && c <= '9') { 
2537     handler.on_precision(parse_nonnegative_int(begin, end, handler)); 
2538   } else if (c == '{') { 
2539     ++begin; 
2540     if (begin != end) { 
2541       begin = 
2542           parse_arg_id(begin, end, precision_adapter<Handler, Char>(handler)); 
2543     } 
2544     if (begin == end || *begin++ != '}') 
2545       return handler.on_error("invalid format string"), begin; 
2546   } else { 
2547     return handler.on_error("missing precision specifier"), begin; 
2548   } 
2549   handler.end_precision(); 
2550   return begin; 
2551 } 
2552  
2553 // Parses standard format specifiers and sends notifications about parsed 
2554 // components to handler. 
2555 template <typename Char, typename SpecHandler> 
2556 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_format_specs(const Char* begin, const Char* end, 
2557                                              SpecHandler&& handler) { 
2558   if (begin == end || *begin == '}') return begin; 
2559  
2560   begin = parse_align(begin, end, handler); 
2561   if (begin == end) return begin; 
2562  
2563   // Parse sign. 
2564   switch (static_cast<char>(*begin)) { 
2565   case '+': 
2566     handler.on_plus(); 
2567     ++begin; 
2568     break; 
2569   case '-': 
2570     handler.on_minus(); 
2571     ++begin; 
2572     break; 
2573   case ' ': 
2574     handler.on_space(); 
2575     ++begin; 
2576     break; 
2577   } 
2578   if (begin == end) return begin; 
2579  
2580   if (*begin == '#') { 
2581     handler.on_hash(); 
2582     if (++begin == end) return begin; 
2583   } 
2584  
2585   // Parse zero flag. 
2586   if (*begin == '0') { 
2587     handler.on_zero(); 
2588     if (++begin == end) return begin; 
2589   } 
2590  
2591   begin = parse_width(begin, end, handler); 
2592   if (begin == end) return begin; 
2593  
2594   // Parse precision. 
2595   if (*begin == '.') { 
2596     begin = parse_precision(begin, end, handler); 
2597   } 
2598  
2599   // Parse type. 
2600   if (begin != end && *begin != '}') handler.on_type(*begin++); 
2601   return begin; 
2602 } 
2603  
2604 // Return the result via the out param to workaround gcc bug 77539. 
2605 template <bool IS_CONSTEXPR, typename T, typename Ptr = const T*> 
2606 FMT_CONSTEXPR bool find(Ptr first, Ptr last, T value, Ptr& out) { 
2607   for (out = first; out != last; ++out) { 
2608     if (*out == value) return true; 
2609   } 
2610   return false; 
2611 } 
2612  
2613 template <> 
2614 inline bool find<false, char>(const char* first, const char* last, char value, 
2615                               const char*& out) { 
2616   out = static_cast<const char*>( 
2617       std::memchr(first, value, detail::to_unsigned(last - first))); 
2618   return out != nullptr; 
2619 } 
2620  
2621 template <typename Handler, typename Char> struct id_adapter { 
2622   Handler& handler; 
2623   int arg_id; 
2624  
2625   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()() { arg_id = handler.on_arg_id(); } 
2626   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(int id) { arg_id = handler.on_arg_id(id); } 
2627   FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(basic_string_view<Char> id) { 
2628     arg_id = handler.on_arg_id(id); 
2629   } 
2630   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_error(const char* message) { 
2631     handler.on_error(message); 
2632   } 
2633 }; 
2634  
2635 template <typename Char, typename Handler> 
2636 FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* parse_replacement_field(const Char* begin, 
2637                                                   const Char* end, 
2638                                                   Handler&& handler) { 
2639   ++begin; 
2640   if (begin == end) return handler.on_error("invalid format string"), end; 
2641   if (static_cast<char>(*begin) == '}') { 
2642     handler.on_replacement_field(handler.on_arg_id(), begin); 
2643   } else if (*begin == '{') { 
2644     handler.on_text(begin, begin + 1); 
2645   } else { 
2646     auto adapter = id_adapter<Handler, Char>{handler, 0}; 
2647     begin = parse_arg_id(begin, end, adapter); 
2648     Char c = begin != end ? *begin : Char(); 
2649     if (c == '}') { 
2650       handler.on_replacement_field(adapter.arg_id, begin); 
2651     } else if (c == ':') { 
2652       begin = handler.on_format_specs(adapter.arg_id, begin + 1, end); 
2653       if (begin == end || *begin != '}') 
2654         return handler.on_error("unknown format specifier"), end; 
2655     } else { 
2656       return handler.on_error("missing '}' in format string"), end; 
2657     } 
2658   } 
2659   return begin + 1; 
2660 } 
2661  
2662 template <bool IS_CONSTEXPR, typename Char, typename Handler> 
2663 FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL FMT_INLINE void parse_format_string( 
2664     basic_string_view<Char> format_str, Handler&& handler) { 
2665   auto begin = format_str.data(); 
2666   auto end = begin + format_str.size(); 
2667   if (end - begin < 32) { 
2668     // Use a simple loop instead of memchr for small strings. 
2669     const Char* p = begin; 
2670     while (p != end) { 
2671       auto c = *p++; 
2672       if (c == '{') { 
2673         handler.on_text(begin, p - 1); 
2674         begin = p = parse_replacement_field(p - 1, end, handler); 
2675       } else if (c == '}') { 
2676         if (p == end || *p != '}') 
2677           return handler.on_error("unmatched '}' in format string"); 
2678         handler.on_text(begin, p); 
2679         begin = ++p; 
2680       } 
2681     } 
2682     handler.on_text(begin, end); 
2683     return; 
2684   } 
2685   struct writer { 
2686     FMT_CONSTEXPR void operator()(const Char* begin, const Char* end) { 
2687       if (begin == end) return; 
2688       for (;;) { 
2689         const Char* p = nullptr; 
2690         if (!find<IS_CONSTEXPR>(begin, end, '}', p)) 
2691           return handler_.on_text(begin, end); 
2692         ++p; 
2693         if (p == end || *p != '}') 
2694           return handler_.on_error("unmatched '}' in format string"); 
2695         handler_.on_text(begin, p); 
2696         begin = p + 1; 
2697       } 
2698     } 
2699     Handler& handler_; 
2700   } write{handler}; 
2701   while (begin != end) { 
2702     // Doing two passes with memchr (one for '{' and another for '}') is up to 
2703     // 2.5x faster than the naive one-pass implementation on big format strings. 
2704     const Char* p = begin; 
2705     if (*begin != '{' && !find<IS_CONSTEXPR>(begin + 1, end, '{', p)) 
2706       return write(begin, end); 
2707     write(begin, p); 
2708     begin = parse_replacement_field(p, end, handler); 
2709   } 
2710 } 
2711  
2712 template <typename T, typename ParseContext> 
2713 FMT_CONSTEXPR const typename ParseContext::char_type* parse_format_specs( 
2714     ParseContext& ctx) { 
2715   using char_type = typename ParseContext::char_type; 
2716   using context = buffer_context<char_type>; 
2717   using mapped_type = 
2718       conditional_t<detail::mapped_type_constant<T, context>::value != 
2719                         type::custom_type, 
2720                     decltype(arg_mapper<context>().map(std::declval<T>())), T>; 
2721   auto f = conditional_t<has_formatter<mapped_type, context>::value, 
2722                          formatter<mapped_type, char_type>, 
2723                          detail::fallback_formatter<T, char_type>>(); 
2724   return f.parse(ctx); 
2725 } 
2726  
2727 template <typename ArgFormatter, typename Char, typename Context> 
2728 struct format_handler : detail::error_handler { 
2729   basic_format_parse_context<Char> parse_context; 
2730   Context context; 
2731  
2732   format_handler(typename ArgFormatter::iterator out, 
2733                  basic_string_view<Char> str, 
2734                  basic_format_args<Context> format_args, detail::locale_ref loc) 
2735       : parse_context(str), context(out, format_args, loc) {} 
2736  
2737   void on_text(const Char* begin, const Char* end) { 
2738     auto size = to_unsigned(end - begin); 
2739     auto out = context.out(); 
2740     auto&& it = reserve(out, size); 
2741     it = std::copy_n(begin, size, it); 
2742     context.advance_to(out); 
2743   } 
2744  
2745   int on_arg_id() { return parse_context.next_arg_id(); } 
2746   int on_arg_id(int id) { return parse_context.check_arg_id(id), id; } 
2747   int on_arg_id(basic_string_view<Char> id) { 
2748     int arg_id = context.arg_id(id); 
2749     if (arg_id < 0) on_error("argument not found"); 
2750     return arg_id; 
2751   } 
2752  
2753   FMT_INLINE void on_replacement_field(int id, const Char*) { 
2754     auto arg = get_arg(context, id); 
2755     context.advance_to(visit_format_arg( 
2756         default_arg_formatter<typename ArgFormatter::iterator, Char>{ 
2757             context.out(), context.args(), context.locale()}, 
2758         arg)); 
2759   } 
2760  
2761   const Char* on_format_specs(int id, const Char* begin, const Char* end) { 
2762     advance_to(parse_context, begin); 
2763     auto arg = get_arg(context, id); 
2764     custom_formatter<Context> f(parse_context, context); 
2765     if (visit_format_arg(f, arg)) return parse_context.begin(); 
2766     basic_format_specs<Char> specs; 
2767     using parse_context_t = basic_format_parse_context<Char>; 
2768     specs_checker<specs_handler<parse_context_t, Context>> handler( 
2769         specs_handler<parse_context_t, Context>(specs, parse_context, context), 
2770         arg.type()); 
2771     begin = parse_format_specs(begin, end, handler); 
2772     if (begin == end || *begin != '}') on_error("missing '}' in format string"); 
2773     advance_to(parse_context, begin); 
2774     context.advance_to( 
2775         visit_format_arg(ArgFormatter(context, &parse_context, &specs), arg)); 
2776     return begin; 
2777   } 
2778 }; 
2779  
2780 // A parse context with extra argument id checks. It is only used at compile 
2781 // time because adding checks at runtime would introduce substantial overhead 
2782 // and would be redundant since argument ids are checked when arguments are 
2783 // retrieved anyway. 
2784 template <typename Char, typename ErrorHandler = error_handler> 
2785 class compile_parse_context 
2786     : public basic_format_parse_context<Char, ErrorHandler> { 
2787  private: 
2788   int num_args_; 
2789   using base = basic_format_parse_context<Char, ErrorHandler>; 
2790  
2791  public: 
2792   explicit FMT_CONSTEXPR compile_parse_context( 
2793       basic_string_view<Char> format_str, int num_args = max_value<int>(), 
2794       ErrorHandler eh = {}) 
2795       : base(format_str, eh), num_args_(num_args) {} 
2796  
2797   FMT_CONSTEXPR int next_arg_id() { 
2798     int id = base::next_arg_id(); 
2799     if (id >= num_args_) this->on_error("argument not found"); 
2800     return id; 
2801   } 
2802  
2803   FMT_CONSTEXPR void check_arg_id(int id) { 
2804     base::check_arg_id(id); 
2805     if (id >= num_args_) this->on_error("argument not found"); 
2806   } 
2807   using base::check_arg_id; 
2808 }; 
2809  
2810 template <typename Char, typename ErrorHandler, typename... Args> 
2811 class format_string_checker { 
2812  public: 
2813   explicit FMT_CONSTEXPR format_string_checker( 
2814       basic_string_view<Char> format_str, ErrorHandler eh) 
2815       : context_(format_str, num_args, eh), 
2816         parse_funcs_{&parse_format_specs<Args, parse_context_type>...} {} 
2817  
2818   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_text(const Char*, const Char*) {} 
2819  
2820   FMT_CONSTEXPR int on_arg_id() { return context_.next_arg_id(); } 
2821   FMT_CONSTEXPR int on_arg_id(int id) { return context_.check_arg_id(id), id; } 
2822   FMT_CONSTEXPR int on_arg_id(basic_string_view<Char>) { 
2823     on_error("compile-time checks don't support named arguments"); 
2824     return 0; 
2825   } 
2826  
2827   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_replacement_field(int, const Char*) {} 
2828  
2829   FMT_CONSTEXPR const Char* on_format_specs(int id, const Char* begin, 
2830                                             const Char*) { 
2831     advance_to(context_, begin); 
2832     return id < num_args ? parse_funcs_[id](context_) : begin; 
2833   } 
2834  
2835   FMT_CONSTEXPR void on_error(const char* message) { 
2836     context_.on_error(message); 
2837   } 
2838  
2839  private: 
2840   using parse_context_type = compile_parse_context<Char, ErrorHandler>; 
2841   enum { num_args = sizeof...(Args) }; 
2842  
2843   // Format specifier parsing function. 
2844   using parse_func = const Char* (*)(parse_context_type&); 
2845  
2846   parse_context_type context_; 
2847   parse_func parse_funcs_[num_args > 0 ? num_args : 1]; 
2848 }; 
2849  
2850 // Converts string literals to basic_string_view. 
2851 template <typename Char, size_t N> 
2852 FMT_CONSTEXPR basic_string_view<Char> compile_string_to_view( 
2853     const Char (&s)[N]) { 
2854   // Remove trailing null character if needed. Won't be present if this is used 
2855   // with raw character array (i.e. not defined as a string). 
2856   return {s, 
2857           N - ((std::char_traits<Char>::to_int_type(s[N - 1]) == 0) ? 1 : 0)}; 
2858 } 
2859  
2860 // Converts string_view to basic_string_view. 
2861 template <typename Char> 
2862 FMT_CONSTEXPR basic_string_view<Char> compile_string_to_view( 
2863     const std_string_view<Char>& s) { 
2864   return {s.data(), s.size()}; 
2865 } 
2866  
2867 #define FMT_STRING_IMPL(s, base)                                  \ 
2868   [] {                                                            \ 
2869     /* Use a macro-like name to avoid shadowing warnings. */      \ 
2870     struct FMT_COMPILE_STRING : base {                            \ 
2871       using char_type = fmt::remove_cvref_t<decltype(s[0])>;      \ 
2872       FMT_MAYBE_UNUSED FMT_CONSTEXPR                              \ 
2873       operator fmt::basic_string_view<char_type>() const {        \ 
2874         return fmt::detail::compile_string_to_view<char_type>(s); \ 
2875       }                                                           \ 
2876     };                                                            \ 
2877     return FMT_COMPILE_STRING();                                  \ 
2878   }() 
2879  
2880 /** 
2881   \rst 
2882   Constructs a compile-time format string from a string literal *s*. 
2883  
2884   **Example**:: 
2885  
2886     // A compile-time error because 'd' is an invalid specifier for strings. 
2887     std::string s = fmt::format(FMT_STRING("{:d}"), "foo"); 
2888   \endrst 
2889  */ 
2890 #define FMT_STRING(s) FMT_STRING_IMPL(s, fmt::compile_string) 
2891  
2892 template <typename... Args, typename S, 
2893           enable_if_t<(is_compile_string<S>::value), int>> 
2894 void check_format_string(S format_str) { 
2895   FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL auto s = to_string_view(format_str); 
2896   using checker = format_string_checker<typename S::char_type, error_handler, 
2897                                         remove_cvref_t<Args>...>; 
2898   FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL bool invalid_format = 
2899       (parse_format_string<true>(s, checker(s, {})), true); 
2900   (void)invalid_format; 
2901 } 
2902  
2903 template <template <typename> class Handler, typename Context> 
2904 void handle_dynamic_spec(int& value, arg_ref<typename Context::char_type> ref, 
2905                          Context& ctx) { 
2906   switch (ref.kind) { 
2907   case arg_id_kind::none: 
2908     break; 
2909   case arg_id_kind::index: 
2910     value = detail::get_dynamic_spec<Handler>(ctx.arg(ref.val.index), 
2911                                               ctx.error_handler()); 
2912     break; 
2913   case arg_id_kind::name: 
2914     value = detail::get_dynamic_spec<Handler>(ctx.arg(ref.val.name), 
2915                                               ctx.error_handler()); 
2916     break; 
2917   } 
2918 }

2921  
2922 FMT_API void format_error_code(buffer<char>& out, int error_code, 
2923                                string_view message) FMT_NOEXCEPT; 
2924  
2925 FMT_API void report_error(format_func func, int error_code, 
2926                           string_view message) FMT_NOEXCEPT;

2928 /** The default argument formatter. */ 
2929 template <typename OutputIt, typename Char> 
2930 class arg_formatter : public arg_formatter_base<OutputIt, Char> { 
2931  private: 
2932   using char_type = Char; 
2933   using base = arg_formatter_base<OutputIt, Char>; 
2934   using context_type = basic_format_context<OutputIt, Char>; 
2935  
2936   context_type& ctx_; 
2937   basic_format_parse_context<char_type>* parse_ctx_; 
2938   const Char* ptr_; 
2939  
2940  public: 
2941   using iterator = typename base::iterator; 
2942   using format_specs = typename base::format_specs; 
2943  
2944   /** 
2945     \rst 
2946     Constructs an argument formatter object. 
2947     *ctx* is a reference to the formatting context, 
2948     *specs* contains format specifier information for standard argument types. 
2949     \endrst 
2950    */ 
2951   explicit arg_formatter( 
2952       context_type& ctx, 
2953       basic_format_parse_context<char_type>* parse_ctx = nullptr, 
2954       format_specs* specs = nullptr, const Char* ptr = nullptr) 
2955       : base(ctx.out(), specs, ctx.locale()), 
2956         ctx_(ctx), 
2957         parse_ctx_(parse_ctx), 
2958         ptr_(ptr) {} 
2959  
2960   using base::operator(); 
2961  
2962   /** Formats an argument of a user-defined type. */ 
2963   iterator operator()(typename basic_format_arg<context_type>::handle handle) { 
2964     if (ptr_) advance_to(*parse_ctx_, ptr_); 
2965     handle.format(*parse_ctx_, ctx_); 
2966     return ctx_.out(); 
2967   } 
2968 }; 
2969 }  // namespace detail 
2970  
2971 template <typename OutputIt, typename Char> 
2972 using arg_formatter FMT_DEPRECATED_ALIAS = 
2973     detail::arg_formatter<OutputIt, Char>;

2975 /** 
2976  An error returned by an operating system or a language runtime, 
2977  for example a file opening error. 
2978 */ 
2979 FMT_CLASS_API 
2980 class FMT_API system_error : public std::runtime_error { 
2981  private: 
2982   void init(int err_code, string_view format_str, format_args args); 
2983  
2984  protected: 
2985   int error_code_; 
2986  
2987   system_error() : std::runtime_error(""), error_code_(0) {} 
2988  
2989  public: 
2990   /** 
2991    \rst 
2992    Constructs a :class:`fmt::system_error` object with a description 
2993    formatted with `fmt::format_system_error`. *message* and additional 
2994    arguments passed into the constructor are formatted similarly to 
2995    `fmt::format`. 
2996  
2997    **Example**:: 
2998  
2999      // This throws a system_error with the description 
3000      //   cannot open file 'madeup': No such file or directory 
3001      // or similar (system message may vary). 
3002      const char *filename = "madeup"; 
3003      std::FILE *file = std::fopen(filename, "r"); 
3004      if (!file) 
3005        throw fmt::system_error(errno, "cannot open file '{}'", filename); 
3006    \endrst 
3007   */ 
3008   template <typename... Args> 
3009   system_error(int error_code, string_view message, const Args&... args) 
3010       : std::runtime_error("") { 
3011     init(error_code, message, make_format_args(args...)); 
3012   } 
3013   system_error(const system_error&) = default; 
3014   system_error& operator=(const system_error&) = default; 
3015   system_error(system_error&&) = default; 
3016   system_error& operator=(system_error&&) = default; 
3017   ~system_error() FMT_NOEXCEPT FMT_OVERRIDE; 
3018  
3019   int error_code() const { return error_code_; } 
3020 }; 
3021  
3022 /** 
3023   \rst 
3024   Formats an error returned by an operating system or a language runtime, 
3025   for example a file opening error, and writes it to *out* in the following 
3026   form: 
3027  
3028   .. parsed-literal:: 
3029      *<message>*: *<system-message>* 
3030  
3031   where *<message>* is the passed message and *<system-message>* is 
3032   the system message corresponding to the error code. 
3033   *error_code* is a system error code as given by ``errno``. 
3034   If *error_code* is not a valid error code such as -1, the system message 
3035   may look like "Unknown error -1" and is platform-dependent. 
3036   \endrst 
3037  */ 
3038 FMT_API void format_system_error(detail::buffer<char>& out, int error_code, 
3039                                  string_view message) FMT_NOEXCEPT; 
3040  
3041 // Reports a system error without throwing an exception. 
3042 // Can be used to report errors from destructors. 
3043 FMT_API void report_system_error(int error_code, 
3044                                  string_view message) FMT_NOEXCEPT; 
3045  
3046 /** Fast integer formatter. */ 
3047 class format_int { 
3048  private: 
3049   // Buffer should be large enough to hold all digits (digits10 + 1), 
3050   // a sign and a null character. 
3051   enum { buffer_size = std::numeric_limits<unsigned long long>::digits10 + 3 }; 
3052   mutable char buffer_[buffer_size]; 
3053   char* str_; 
3054  
3055   template <typename UInt> char* format_unsigned(UInt value) { 
3056     auto n = static_cast<detail::uint32_or_64_or_128_t<UInt>>(value); 
3057     return detail::format_decimal(buffer_, n, buffer_size - 1).begin; 
3058   } 
3059  
3060   template <typename Int> char* format_signed(Int value) { 
3061     auto abs_value = static_cast<detail::uint32_or_64_or_128_t<Int>>(value); 
3062     bool negative = value < 0; 
3063     if (negative) abs_value = 0 - abs_value; 
3064     auto begin = format_unsigned(abs_value); 
3065     if (negative) *--begin = '-'; 
3066     return begin; 
3067   } 
3068  
3069  public: 
3070   explicit format_int(int value) : str_(format_signed(value)) {} 
3071   explicit format_int(long value) : str_(format_signed(value)) {} 
3072   explicit format_int(long long value) : str_(format_signed(value)) {} 
3073   explicit format_int(unsigned value) : str_(format_unsigned(value)) {} 
3074   explicit format_int(unsigned long value) : str_(format_unsigned(value)) {} 
3075   explicit format_int(unsigned long long value) 
3076       : str_(format_unsigned(value)) {} 
3077  
3078   /** Returns the number of characters written to the output buffer. */ 
3079   size_t size() const { 
3080     return detail::to_unsigned(buffer_ - str_ + buffer_size - 1); 
3081   } 
3082  
3083   /** 
3084     Returns a pointer to the output buffer content. No terminating null 
3085     character is appended. 
3086    */ 
3087   const char* data() const { return str_; } 
3088  
3089   /** 
3090     Returns a pointer to the output buffer content with terminating null 
3091     character appended. 
3092    */ 
3093   const char* c_str() const { 
3094     buffer_[buffer_size - 1] = '\0'; 
3095     return str_; 
3096   } 
3097  
3098   /** 
3099     \rst 
3100     Returns the content of the output buffer as an ``std::string``. 
3101     \endrst 
3102    */ 
3103   std::string str() const { return std::string(str_, size()); } 
3104 }; 
3105  
3106 // A formatter specialization for the core types corresponding to detail::type 
3107 // constants. 
3108 template <typename T, typename Char> 
3109 struct formatter<T, Char, 
3110                  enable_if_t<detail::type_constant<T, Char>::value != 
3111                              detail::type::custom_type>> { 
3112   FMT_CONSTEXPR formatter() = default; 
3113  
3114   // Parses format specifiers stopping either at the end of the range or at the 
3115   // terminating '}'. 
3116   template <typename ParseContext> 
3117   FMT_CONSTEXPR auto parse(ParseContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.begin()) { 
3118     using handler_type = detail::dynamic_specs_handler<ParseContext>; 
3119     auto type = detail::type_constant<T, Char>::value; 
3120     detail::specs_checker<handler_type> handler(handler_type(specs_, ctx), 
3121                                                 type); 
3122     auto it = parse_format_specs(ctx.begin(), ctx.end(), handler); 
3123     auto eh = ctx.error_handler(); 
3124     switch (type) { 
3125     case detail::type::none_type: 
3126       FMT_ASSERT(false, "invalid argument type"); 
3127       break; 
3128     case detail::type::int_type: 
3129     case detail::type::uint_type: 
3130     case detail::type::long_long_type: 
3131     case detail::type::ulong_long_type: 
3132     case detail::type::int128_type: 
3133     case detail::type::uint128_type: 
3134     case detail::type::bool_type: 
3135       handle_int_type_spec(specs_.type, 
3136                            detail::int_type_checker<decltype(eh)>(eh)); 
3137       break; 
3138     case detail::type::char_type: 
3139       handle_char_specs( 
3140           &specs_, detail::char_specs_checker<decltype(eh)>(specs_.type, eh)); 
3141       break; 
3142     case detail::type::float_type: 
3143       if (detail::const_check(FMT_USE_FLOAT)) 
3144         detail::parse_float_type_spec(specs_, eh); 
3145       else 
3146         FMT_ASSERT(false, "float support disabled"); 
3147       break; 
3148     case detail::type::double_type: 
3149       if (detail::const_check(FMT_USE_DOUBLE)) 
3150         detail::parse_float_type_spec(specs_, eh); 
3151       else 
3152         FMT_ASSERT(false, "double support disabled"); 
3153       break; 
3154     case detail::type::long_double_type: 
3155       if (detail::const_check(FMT_USE_LONG_DOUBLE)) 
3156         detail::parse_float_type_spec(specs_, eh); 
3157       else 
3158         FMT_ASSERT(false, "long double support disabled"); 
3159       break; 
3160     case detail::type::cstring_type: 
3161       detail::handle_cstring_type_spec( 
3162           specs_.type, detail::cstring_type_checker<decltype(eh)>(eh)); 
3163       break; 
3164     case detail::type::string_type: 
3165       detail::check_string_type_spec(specs_.type, eh); 
3166       break; 
3167     case detail::type::pointer_type: 
3168       detail::check_pointer_type_spec(specs_.type, eh); 
3169       break; 
3170     case detail::type::custom_type: 
3171       // Custom format specifiers should be checked in parse functions of 
3172       // formatter specializations. 
3173       break; 
3174     } 
3175     return it; 
3176   } 
3177  
3178   template <typename FormatContext> 
3179   auto format(const T& val, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3180     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::width_checker>(specs_.width, 
3181                                                        specs_.width_ref, ctx); 
3182     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::precision_checker>( 
3183         specs_.precision, specs_.precision_ref, ctx); 
3184     using af = detail::arg_formatter<typename FormatContext::iterator, 
3185                                      typename FormatContext::char_type>; 
3186     return visit_format_arg(af(ctx, nullptr, &specs_), 
3187                             detail::make_arg<FormatContext>(val)); 
3188   } 
3189  
3190  private: 
3191   detail::dynamic_format_specs<Char> specs_; 
3192 }; 
3193  
3194 #define FMT_FORMAT_AS(Type, Base)                                             \ 
3195   template <typename Char>                                                    \ 
3196   struct formatter<Type, Char> : formatter<Base, Char> {                      \ 
3197     template <typename FormatContext>                                         \ 
3198     auto format(Type const& val, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { \ 
3199       return formatter<Base, Char>::format(val, ctx);                         \ 
3200     }                                                                         \ 
3201   } 
3202  
3203 FMT_FORMAT_AS(signed char, int); 
3204 FMT_FORMAT_AS(unsigned char, unsigned); 
3205 FMT_FORMAT_AS(short, int); 
3206 FMT_FORMAT_AS(unsigned short, unsigned); 
3207 FMT_FORMAT_AS(long, long long); 
3208 FMT_FORMAT_AS(unsigned long, unsigned long long); 
3209 FMT_FORMAT_AS(Char*, const Char*); 
3210 FMT_FORMAT_AS(std::basic_string<Char>, basic_string_view<Char>); 
3211 FMT_FORMAT_AS(std::nullptr_t, const void*); 
3212 FMT_FORMAT_AS(detail::std_string_view<Char>, basic_string_view<Char>);

3215 struct formatter<void*, Char> : formatter<const void*, Char> { 
3216   template <typename FormatContext> 
3217   auto format(void* val, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3218     return formatter<const void*, Char>::format(val, ctx);

3223 struct formatter<Char[N], Char> : formatter<basic_string_view<Char>, Char> { 
3224   template <typename FormatContext> 
3225   auto format(const Char* val, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3226     return formatter<basic_string_view<Char>, Char>::format(val, ctx);

3230 // A formatter for types known only at run time such as variant alternatives. 
3231 // 
3232 // Usage: 
3233 //   using variant = std::variant<int, std::string>; 
3234 //   template <> 
3235 //   struct formatter<variant>: dynamic_formatter<> { 
3236 //     void format(buffer &buf, const variant &v, context &ctx) { 
3237 //       visit([&](const auto &val) { format(buf, val, ctx); }, v); 
3238 //     } 
3239 //   }; 
3240 template <typename Char = char> class dynamic_formatter { 
3241  private: 
3242   struct null_handler : detail::error_handler { 
3243     void on_align(align_t) {} 
3244     void on_plus() {} 
3245     void on_minus() {} 
3246     void on_space() {} 
3247     void on_hash() {} 
3248   }; 
3249  
3250  public: 
3251   template <typename ParseContext> 
3252   auto parse(ParseContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.begin()) { 
3253     format_str_ = ctx.begin(); 
3254     // Checks are deferred to formatting time when the argument type is known. 
3255     detail::dynamic_specs_handler<ParseContext> handler(specs_, ctx); 
3256     return parse_format_specs(ctx.begin(), ctx.end(), handler); 
3257   } 
3258  
3259   template <typename T, typename FormatContext> 
3260   auto format(const T& val, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3261     handle_specs(ctx); 
3262     detail::specs_checker<null_handler> checker( 
3263         null_handler(), detail::mapped_type_constant<T, FormatContext>::value); 
3264     checker.on_align(specs_.align); 
3265     switch (specs_.sign) { 
3266     case sign::none: 
3267       break; 
3268     case sign::plus: 
3269       checker.on_plus(); 
3270       break; 
3271     case sign::minus: 
3272       checker.on_minus(); 
3273       break; 
3274     case sign::space: 
3275       checker.on_space(); 
3276       break; 
3277     } 
3278     if (specs_.alt) checker.on_hash(); 
3279     if (specs_.precision >= 0) checker.end_precision(); 
3280     using af = detail::arg_formatter<typename FormatContext::iterator, 
3281                                      typename FormatContext::char_type>; 
3282     visit_format_arg(af(ctx, nullptr, &specs_), 
3283                      detail::make_arg<FormatContext>(val)); 
3284     return ctx.out(); 
3285   } 
3286  
3287  private: 
3288   template <typename Context> void handle_specs(Context& ctx) { 
3289     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::width_checker>(specs_.width, 
3290                                                        specs_.width_ref, ctx); 
3291     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::precision_checker>( 
3292         specs_.precision, specs_.precision_ref, ctx); 
3293   } 
3294  
3295   detail::dynamic_format_specs<Char> specs_; 
3296   const Char* format_str_; 
3297 }; 
3298  
3299 template <typename Char, typename ErrorHandler> 
3300 FMT_CONSTEXPR void advance_to( 
3301     basic_format_parse_context<Char, ErrorHandler>& ctx, const Char* p) { 
3302   ctx.advance_to(ctx.begin() + (p - &*ctx.begin())); 
3303 } 
3304  
3305 /** Formats arguments and writes the output to the range. */ 
3306 template <typename ArgFormatter, typename Char, typename Context> 
3307 typename Context::iterator vformat_to( 
3308     typename ArgFormatter::iterator out, basic_string_view<Char> format_str, 
3309     basic_format_args<Context> args, 
3310     detail::locale_ref loc = detail::locale_ref()) { 
3311   if (format_str.size() == 2 && detail::equal2(format_str.data(), "{}")) { 
3312     auto arg = args.get(0); 
3313     if (!arg) detail::error_handler().on_error("argument not found"); 
3314     using iterator = typename ArgFormatter::iterator; 
3315     return visit_format_arg( 
3316         detail::default_arg_formatter<iterator, Char>{out, args, loc}, arg); 
3317   } 
3318   detail::format_handler<ArgFormatter, Char, Context> h(out, format_str, args, 
3319                                                         loc); 
3320   detail::parse_format_string<false>(format_str, h); 
3321   return h.context.out(); 
3322 }

3324 // Casts ``p`` to ``const void*`` for pointer formatting. 
3325 // Example: 
3326 //   auto s = format("{}", ptr(p)); 
3327 template <typename T> inline const void* ptr(const T* p) { return p; } 
3328 template <typename T> inline const void* ptr(const std::unique_ptr<T>& p) { 
3329   return p.get(); 
3330 } 
3331 template <typename T> inline const void* ptr(const std::shared_ptr<T>& p) { 
3332   return p.get(); 
3333 }

3335 class bytes { 
3336  private: 
3337   string_view data_; 
3338   friend struct formatter<bytes>; 
3339  
3340  public: 
3341   explicit bytes(string_view data) : data_(data) {} 
3342 }; 
3343  
3344 template <> struct formatter<bytes> { 
3345  private: 
3346   detail::dynamic_format_specs<char> specs_; 
3347  
3348  public: 
3349   template <typename ParseContext> 
3350   FMT_CONSTEXPR auto parse(ParseContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.begin()) { 
3351     using handler_type = detail::dynamic_specs_handler<ParseContext>; 
3352     detail::specs_checker<handler_type> handler(handler_type(specs_, ctx), 
3353                                                 detail::type::string_type); 
3354     auto it = parse_format_specs(ctx.begin(), ctx.end(), handler); 
3355     detail::check_string_type_spec(specs_.type, ctx.error_handler()); 
3356     return it; 
3357   } 
3358  
3359   template <typename FormatContext> 
3360   auto format(bytes b, FormatContext& ctx) -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3361     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::width_checker>(specs_.width, 
3362                                                        specs_.width_ref, ctx); 
3363     detail::handle_dynamic_spec<detail::precision_checker>( 
3364         specs_.precision, specs_.precision_ref, ctx); 
3365     return detail::write_bytes(ctx.out(), b.data_, specs_); 
3366   } 
3367 };

3369 template <typename It, typename Sentinel, typename Char> 
3370 struct arg_join : detail::view { 
3371   It begin; 
3372   Sentinel end; 
3373   basic_string_view<Char> sep; 
3374  
3375   arg_join(It b, Sentinel e, basic_string_view<Char> s) 
3376       : begin(b), end(e), sep(s) {} 
3377 }; 
3378  
3379 template <typename It, typename Sentinel, typename Char> 
3380 struct formatter<arg_join<It, Sentinel, Char>, Char> 
3381     : formatter<typename std::iterator_traits<It>::value_type, Char> { 
3382   template <typename FormatContext> 
3383   auto format(const arg_join<It, Sentinel, Char>& value, FormatContext& ctx) 
3384       -> decltype(ctx.out()) { 
3385     using base = formatter<typename std::iterator_traits<It>::value_type, Char>; 
3386     auto it = value.begin; 
3387     auto out = ctx.out(); 
3388     if (it != value.end) { 
3389       out = base::format(*it++, ctx); 
3390       while (it != value.end) { 
3391         out = std::copy(value.sep.begin(), value.sep.end(), out); 
3392         ctx.advance_to(out); 
3393         out = base::format(*it++, ctx); 
3394       } 
3395     } 
3396     return out; 
3397   } 
3398 };

3400 /** 
3401   Returns an object that formats the iterator range `[begin, end)` with elements 
3402   separated by `sep`. 
3403  */ 
3404 template <typename It, typename Sentinel> 
3405 arg_join<It, Sentinel, char> join(It begin, Sentinel end, string_view sep) { 
3406   return {begin, end, sep};

3410 arg_join<It, Sentinel, wchar_t> join(It begin, Sentinel end, wstring_view sep) {

3414 /** 
3415   \rst 
3416   Returns an object that formats `range` with elements separated by `sep`. 
3417  
3418   **Example**:: 
3419  
3420     std::vector<int> v = {1, 2, 3}; 
3421     fmt::print("{}", fmt::join(v, ", ")); 
3422     // Output: "1, 2, 3" 
3423  
3424   ``fmt::join`` applies passed format specifiers to the range elements:: 
3425  
3426     fmt::print("{:02}", fmt::join(v, ", ")); 
3427     // Output: "01, 02, 03" 
3428   \endrst 
3429  */ 
3430 template <typename Range> 
3431 arg_join<detail::iterator_t<Range>, detail::sentinel_t<Range>, char> join( 
3432     Range&& range, string_view sep) { 
3433   return join(std::begin(range), std::end(range), sep); 
3434 }

3436 template <typename Range> 
3437 arg_join<detail::iterator_t<Range>, detail::sentinel_t<Range>, wchar_t> join( 
3438     Range&& range, wstring_view sep) { 
3439   return join(std::begin(range), std::end(range), sep); 
3440 }

3442 /** 
3443   \rst 
3444   Converts *value* to ``std::string`` using the default format for type *T*. 
3445  
3446   **Example**:: 
3447  
3448     #include <fmt/format.h> 
3449  
3450     std::string answer = fmt::to_string(42); 
3451   \endrst 
3452  */ 
3453 template <typename T, FMT_ENABLE_IF(!std::is_integral<T>::value)> 
3454 inline std::string to_string(const T& value) { 
3455   std::string result; 
3456   detail::write<char>(std::back_inserter(result), value); 
3457   return result; 
3458 } 
3459  
3460 template <typename T, FMT_ENABLE_IF(std::is_integral<T>::value)> 
3461 inline std::string to_string(T value) { 
3462   // The buffer should be large enough to store the number including the sign or 
3463   // "false" for bool. 
3464   constexpr int max_size = detail::digits10<T>() + 2; 
3465   char buffer[max_size > 5 ? max_size : 5]; 
3466   char* begin = buffer; 
3467   return std::string(begin, detail::write<char>(begin, value)); 
3468 } 
3469  
3470 /** 
3471   Converts *value* to ``std::wstring`` using the default format for type *T*. 
3472  */ 
3473 template <typename T> inline std::wstring to_wstring(const T& value) { 
3474   return format(L"{}", value); 
3475 } 
3476  
3477 template <typename Char, size_t SIZE> 
3478 std::basic_string<Char> to_string(const basic_memory_buffer<Char, SIZE>& buf) { 
3479   auto size = buf.size(); 
3480   detail::assume(size < std::basic_string<Char>().max_size()); 
3481   return std::basic_string<Char>(buf.data(), size); 
3482 }

3484 template <typename Char> 
3485 detail::buffer_appender<Char> detail::vformat_to( 
3486     detail::buffer<Char>& buf, basic_string_view<Char> format_str, 
3487     basic_format_args<buffer_context<type_identity_t<Char>>> args) { 
3488   using af = arg_formatter<typename buffer_context<Char>::iterator, Char>; 
3489   return vformat_to<af>(buffer_appender<Char>(buf), format_str, args); 
3490 }

3528           typename Char = enable_if_t<detail::is_string<S>::value, char_t<S>>>

3531   const auto& vargs = fmt::make_args_checked<Args...>(format_str, args...); 
3532   return detail::vformat_to(buf, to_string_view(format_str), vargs);

3541 template <typename OutputIt, typename Char = typename OutputIt::value_type>

3544 template <typename OutputIt, typename Char = typename OutputIt::value_type> 
3545 using format_to_n_args FMT_DEPRECATED_ALIAS = 
3546     basic_format_args<buffer_context<Char>>; 
3547  
3548 template <typename OutputIt, typename Char, typename... Args> 
3549 FMT_DEPRECATED format_arg_store<buffer_context<Char>, Args...> 
3550 make_format_to_n_args(const Args&... args) { 
3551   return format_arg_store<buffer_context<Char>, Args...>(args...); 
3552 } 
3553  
3554 template <typename Char, enable_if_t<(!std::is_same<Char, char>::value), int>> 
3555 std::basic_string<Char> detail::vformat( 
3556     basic_string_view<Char> format_str, 
3557     basic_format_args<buffer_context<type_identity_t<Char>>> args) { 
3558   basic_memory_buffer<Char> buffer; 
3559   detail::vformat_to(buffer, format_str, args); 
3560   return to_string(buffer); 
3561 } 
3562  
3563 template <typename Char, FMT_ENABLE_IF(std::is_same<Char, wchar_t>::value)> 
3564 void vprint(std::FILE* f, basic_string_view<Char> format_str, 
3565             wformat_args args) { 
3566   wmemory_buffer buffer; 
3567   detail::vformat_to(buffer, format_str, args); 
3568   buffer.push_back(L'\0'); 
3569   if (std::fputws(buffer.data(), f) == -1) 
3570     FMT_THROW(system_error(errno, "cannot write to file")); 
3571 } 
3572  
3573 template <typename Char, FMT_ENABLE_IF(std::is_same<Char, wchar_t>::value)> 
3574 void vprint(basic_string_view<Char> format_str, wformat_args args) { 
3575   vprint(stdout, format_str, args); 
3576 }

3583  public: 
3584   template <typename... Args> 
3585   std::basic_string<Char> operator()(Args&&... args) const { 
3586     static FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL Char s[] = {CHARS..., '\0'}; 
3587     return format(FMT_STRING(s), std::forward<Args>(args)...); 
3588   }

3596     return format(str, std::forward<Args>(args)...); 
3597   }

3602   const Char* str;

3606   }

3610 inline namespace literals { 
3611 #  if FMT_USE_UDL_TEMPLATE 
3612 #    pragma GCC diagnostic push 
3613 #    pragma GCC diagnostic ignored "-Wpedantic" 
3614 #    if FMT_CLANG_VERSION 
3615 #      pragma GCC diagnostic ignored "-Wgnu-string-literal-operator-template" 
3616 #    endif 
3617 template <typename Char, Char... CHARS> 
3618 FMT_CONSTEXPR detail::udl_formatter<Char, CHARS...> operator""_format() { 
3619   return {}; 
3620 } 
3621 #    pragma GCC diagnostic pop 
3622 #  else 
3623 /** 
3624   \rst 
3625   User-defined literal equivalent of :func:`fmt::format`. 
3626  
3627   **Example**:: 
3628  
3629     using namespace fmt::literals; 
3630     std::string message = "The answer is {}"_format(42); 
3631   \endrst 
3632  */ 
3633 FMT_CONSTEXPR detail::udl_formatter<char> operator"" _format(const char* s, 
3634                                                              size_t n) { 
3635   return {{s, n}}; 
3636 } 
3637 FMT_CONSTEXPR detail::udl_formatter<wchar_t> operator"" _format( 
3638     const wchar_t* s, size_t n) { 
3639   return {{s, n}}; 
3640 } 
3641 #  endif  // FMT_USE_UDL_TEMPLATE 
3642  
3643 /** 
3644   \rst 
3645   User-defined literal equivalent of :func:`fmt::arg`. 
3646  
3647   **Example**:: 
3648  
3649     using namespace fmt::literals; 
3650     fmt::print("Elapsed time: {s:.2f} seconds", "s"_a=1.23); 
3651   \endrst 
3652  */ 
3653 FMT_CONSTEXPR detail::udl_arg<char> operator"" _a(const char* s, size_t) { 
3654   return {s}; 
3655 } 
3656 FMT_CONSTEXPR detail::udl_arg<wchar_t> operator"" _a(const wchar_t* s, size_t) { 
3657   return {s}; 
3658 } 
3659 }  // namespace literals

 101 template <typename Exception> inline void do_throw(const Exception& x) { 
 102   // Silence unreachable code warnings in MSVC and NVCC because these 
 103   // are nearly impossible to fix in a generic code. 
 104   volatile bool b = true; 
 105   if (b) throw x; 
 106 } 
 107 }  // namespace detail 
 108 FMT_END_NAMESPACE 
 109 #      define FMT_THROW(x) detail::do_throw(x) 
 110 #    else 
 111 #      define FMT_THROW(x) throw x 
 112 #    endif 
 113 #  else 
 114 #    define FMT_THROW(x)              \ 
 115       do {                            \ 
 116         static_cast<void>(sizeof(x)); \ 
 117         FMT_ASSERT(false, "");        \ 
 118       } while (false) 
 119 #  endif 
 120 #endif

 174 // __builtin_clz is broken in clang with Microsoft CodeGen: 
 175 // https://github.com/fmtlib/fmt/issues/519 
 176 #if (FMT_GCC_VERSION || FMT_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_clz)) && !FMT_MSC_VER 
 177 #  define FMT_BUILTIN_CLZ(n) __builtin_clz(n) 
 178 #endif 
 179 #if (FMT_GCC_VERSION || FMT_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_clzll)) && !FMT_MSC_VER 
 180 #  define FMT_BUILTIN_CLZLL(n) __builtin_clzll(n) 
 181 #endif 
 182  
 183 // Some compilers masquerade as both MSVC and GCC-likes or otherwise support 
 184 // __builtin_clz and __builtin_clzll, so only define FMT_BUILTIN_CLZ using the 
 185 // MSVC intrinsics if the clz and clzll builtins are not available. 
 186 #if FMT_MSC_VER && !defined(FMT_BUILTIN_CLZLL) && !defined(_MANAGED) 
 187 #  include <intrin.h>  // _BitScanReverse, _BitScanReverse64 
 188  
 189 FMT_BEGIN_NAMESPACE 
 190 namespace detail { 
 191 // Avoid Clang with Microsoft CodeGen's -Wunknown-pragmas warning. 
 192 #  ifndef __clang__ 
 193 #    pragma intrinsic(_BitScanReverse) 
 194 #  endif 
 195 inline uint32_t clz(uint32_t x) { 
 196   unsigned long r = 0; 
 197   _BitScanReverse(&r, x); 
 198  
 199   FMT_ASSERT(x != 0, ""); 
 200   // Static analysis complains about using uninitialized data 
 201   // "r", but the only way that can happen is if "x" is 0, 
 202   // which the callers guarantee to not happen. 
 203   FMT_SUPPRESS_MSC_WARNING(6102) 
 204   return 31 - r; 
 205 } 
 206 #  define FMT_BUILTIN_CLZ(n) detail::clz(n) 
 207  
 208 #  if defined(_WIN64) && !defined(__clang__) 
 209 #    pragma intrinsic(_BitScanReverse64) 
 210 #  endif 
 211  
 212 inline uint32_t clzll(uint64_t x) { 
 213   unsigned long r = 0; 
 214 #  ifdef _WIN64 
 215   _BitScanReverse64(&r, x); 
 216 #  else 
 217   // Scan the high 32 bits. 
 218   if (_BitScanReverse(&r, static_cast<uint32_t>(x >> 32))) return 63 - (r + 32); 
 219  
 220   // Scan the low 32 bits. 
 221   _BitScanReverse(&r, static_cast<uint32_t>(x)); 
 222 #  endif 
 223  
 224   FMT_ASSERT(x != 0, ""); 
 225   // Static analysis complains about using uninitialized data 
 226   // "r", but the only way that can happen is if "x" is 0, 
 227   // which the callers guarantee to not happen. 
 228   FMT_SUPPRESS_MSC_WARNING(6102) 
 229   return 63 - r; 
 230 } 
 231 #  define FMT_BUILTIN_CLZLL(n) detail::clzll(n) 
 232 }  // namespace detail 
 233 FMT_END_NAMESPACE 
 234 #endif

 244 // An equivalent of `*reinterpret_cast<Dest*>(&source)` that doesn't have 
 245 // undefined behavior (e.g. due to type aliasing). 
 246 // Example: uint64_t d = bit_cast<uint64_t>(2.718); 
 247 template <typename Dest, typename Source> 
 248 inline Dest bit_cast(const Source& source) { 
 249   static_assert(sizeof(Dest) == sizeof(Source), "size mismatch"); 
 250   Dest dest; 
 251   std::memcpy(&dest, &source, sizeof(dest)); 
 252   return dest; 
 253 } 
 254  
 255 inline bool is_big_endian() { 
 256   const auto u = 1u; 
 257   struct bytes { 
 258     char data[sizeof(u)]; 
 259   }; 
 260   return bit_cast<bytes>(u).data[0] == 0; 
 261 } 
 262  
 263 // A fallback implementation of uintptr_t for systems that lack it. 
 264 struct fallback_uintptr { 
 265   unsigned char value[sizeof(void*)]; 
 266  
 267   fallback_uintptr() = default; 
 268   explicit fallback_uintptr(const void* p) { 
 269     *this = bit_cast<fallback_uintptr>(p); 
 270     if (is_big_endian()) { 
 271       for (size_t i = 0, j = sizeof(void*) - 1; i < j; ++i, --j) 
 272         std::swap(value[i], value[j]); 
 273     } 
 274   } 
 275 }; 
 276 #ifdef UINTPTR_MAX 
 277 using uintptr_t = ::uintptr_t; 
 278 inline uintptr_t to_uintptr(const void* p) { return bit_cast<uintptr_t>(p); } 
 279 #else 
 280 using uintptr_t = fallback_uintptr; 
 281 inline fallback_uintptr to_uintptr(const void* p) { 
 282   return fallback_uintptr(p); 
 283 } 
 284 #endif

 286 // Returns the largest possible value for type T. Same as 
 287 // std::numeric_limits<T>::max() but shorter and not affected by the max macro. 
 288 template <typename T> constexpr T max_value() { 
 289   return (std::numeric_limits<T>::max)(); 
 290 } 
 291 template <typename T> constexpr int num_bits() { 
 292   return std::numeric_limits<T>::digits; 
 293 } 
 294 // std::numeric_limits<T>::digits may return 0 for 128-bit ints. 
 295 template <> constexpr int num_bits<int128_t>() { return 128; } 
 296 template <> constexpr int num_bits<uint128_t>() { return 128; } 
 297 template <> constexpr int num_bits<fallback_uintptr>() { 
 298   return static_cast<int>(sizeof(void*) * 
 299                           std::numeric_limits<unsigned char>::digits); 
 300 }

 302 FMT_INLINE void assume(bool condition) { 
 303   (void)condition; 
 304 #if FMT_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_assume) 
 305   __builtin_assume(condition); 
 306 #endif 
 307 }

 309 // An approximation of iterator_t for pre-C++20 systems. 
 310 template <typename T> 
 311 using iterator_t = decltype(std::begin(std::declval<T&>())); 
 312 template <typename T> using sentinel_t = decltype(std::end(std::declval<T&>()));

 314 // A workaround for std::string not having mutable data() until C++17. 
 315 template <typename Char> inline Char* get_data(std::basic_string<Char>& s) { 
 316   return &s[0]; 
 317 } 
 318 template <typename Container> 
 319 inline typename Container::value_type* get_data(Container& c) { 
 320   return c.data(); 
 321 }

 323 #if defined(_SECURE_SCL) && _SECURE_SCL 
 324 // Make a checked iterator to avoid MSVC warnings. 
 325 template <typename T> using checked_ptr = stdext::checked_array_iterator<T*>; 
 326 template <typename T> checked_ptr<T> make_checked(T* p, size_t size) { 
 327   return {p, size}; 
 328 } 
 329 #else 
 330 template <typename T> using checked_ptr = T*; 
 331 template <typename T> inline T* make_checked(T* p, size_t) { return p; } 
 332 #endif 
 333  
 334 template <typename Container, FMT_ENABLE_IF(is_contiguous<Container>::value)> 
 335 #if FMT_CLANG_VERSION 
 336 __attribute__((no_sanitize("undefined"))) 
 337 #endif 
 338 inline checked_ptr<typename Container::value_type> 
 339 reserve(std::back_insert_iterator<Container> it, size_t n) { 
 340   Container& c = get_container(it); 
 341   size_t size = c.size(); 
 342   c.resize(size + n); 
 343   return make_checked(get_data(c) + size, n); 
 344 } 
 345  
 346 template <typename T> 
 347 inline buffer_appender<T> reserve(buffer_appender<T> it, size_t n) { 
 348   buffer<T>& buf = get_container(it); 
 349   buf.try_reserve(buf.size() + n); 
 350   return it; 
 351 } 
 352  
 353 template <typename Iterator> inline Iterator& reserve(Iterator& it, size_t) { 
 354   return it; 
 355 } 
 356  
 357 template <typename T, typename OutputIt> 
 358 constexpr T* to_pointer(OutputIt, size_t) { 
 359   return nullptr; 
 360 } 
 361 template <typename T> T* to_pointer(buffer_appender<T> it, size_t n) { 
 362   buffer<T>& buf = get_container(it); 
 363   auto size = buf.size(); 
 364   if (buf.capacity() < size + n) return nullptr; 
 365   buf.try_resize(size + n); 
 366   return buf.data() + size; 
 367 }

 369 template <typename Container, FMT_ENABLE_IF(is_contiguous<Container>::value)> 
 370 inline std::back_insert_iterator<Container> base_iterator( 
 371     std::back_insert_iterator<Container>& it, 
 372     checked_ptr<typename Container::value_type>) { 
 373   return it; 
 374 }

 376 template <typename Iterator> 
 377 inline Iterator base_iterator(Iterator, Iterator it) { 
 378   return it; 
 379 }

 381 // An output iterator that counts the number of objects written to it and 
 382 // discards them. 
 383 class counting_iterator { 
 384  private: 
 385   size_t count_; 
 386  
 387  public: 
 388   using iterator_category = std::output_iterator_tag; 
 389   using difference_type = std::ptrdiff_t; 
 390   using pointer = void; 
 391   using reference = void; 
 392   using _Unchecked_type = counting_iterator;  // Mark iterator as checked. 
 393  
 394   struct value_type { 
 395     template <typename T> void operator=(const T&) {} 
 396   }; 
 397  
 398   counting_iterator() : count_(0) {} 
 399  
 400   size_t count() const { return count_; } 
 401  
 402   counting_iterator& operator++() { 
 403     ++count_; 
 404     return *this; 
 405   } 
 406  
 407   counting_iterator operator++(int) { 
 408     auto it = *this; 
 409     ++*this; 
 410     return it; 
 411   } 
 412  
 413   value_type operator*() const { return {}; } 
 414 }; 
 415  
 416 template <typename OutputIt> class truncating_iterator_base { 
 417  protected: 
 418   OutputIt out_; 
 419   size_t limit_; 
 420   size_t count_; 
 421  
 422   truncating_iterator_base(OutputIt out, size_t limit) 
 423       : out_(out), limit_(limit), count_(0) {} 
 424  
 425  public: 
 426   using iterator_category = std::output_iterator_tag; 
 427   using value_type = typename std::iterator_traits<OutputIt>::value_type; 
 428   using difference_type = void; 
 429   using pointer = void; 
 430   using reference = void; 
 431   using _Unchecked_type = 
 432       truncating_iterator_base;  // Mark iterator as checked. 
 433  
 434   OutputIt base() const { return out_; } 
 435   size_t count() const { return count_; } 
 436 }; 
 437  
 438 // An output iterator that truncates the output and counts the number of objects 
 439 // written to it. 
 440 template <typename OutputIt, 
 441           typename Enable = typename std::is_void< 
 442               typename std::iterator_traits<OutputIt>::value_type>::type> 
 443 class truncating_iterator; 
 444  
 445 template <typename OutputIt> 
 446 class truncating_iterator<OutputIt, std::false_type> 
 447     : public truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt> { 
 448   mutable typename truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt>::value_type blackhole_; 
 449  
 450  public: 
 451   using value_type = typename truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt>::value_type; 
 452  
 453   truncating_iterator(OutputIt out, size_t limit) 
 454       : truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt>(out, limit) {} 
 455  
 456   truncating_iterator& operator++() { 
 457     if (this->count_++ < this->limit_) ++this->out_; 
 458     return *this; 
 459   } 
 460  
 461   truncating_iterator operator++(int) { 
 462     auto it = *this; 
 463     ++*this; 
 464     return it; 
 465   } 
 466  
 467   value_type& operator*() const { 
 468     return this->count_ < this->limit_ ? *this->out_ : blackhole_; 
 469   } 
 470 }; 
 471  
 472 template <typename OutputIt> 
 473 class truncating_iterator<OutputIt, std::true_type> 
 474     : public truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt> { 
 475  public: 
 476   truncating_iterator(OutputIt out, size_t limit) 
 477       : truncating_iterator_base<OutputIt>(out, limit) {} 
 478  
 479   template <typename T> truncating_iterator& operator=(T val) { 
 480     if (this->count_++ < this->limit_) *this->out_++ = val; 
 481     return *this; 
 482   } 
 483  
 484   truncating_iterator& operator++() { return *this; } 
 485   truncating_iterator& operator++(int) { return *this; } 
 486   truncating_iterator& operator*() { return *this; } 
 487 }; 
 488  
 489 template <typename Char> 
 490 inline size_t count_code_points(basic_string_view<Char> s) { 
 491   return s.size(); 
 492 } 
 493  
 494 // Counts the number of code points in a UTF-8 string. 
 495 inline size_t count_code_points(basic_string_view<char> s) { 
 496   const char* data = s.data(); 
 497   size_t num_code_points = 0; 
 498   for (size_t i = 0, size = s.size(); i != size; ++i) { 
 499     if ((data[i] & 0xc0) != 0x80) ++num_code_points; 
 500   } 
 501   return num_code_points; 
 502 } 
 503  
 504 inline size_t count_code_points(basic_string_view<char8_type> s) { 
 505   return count_code_points(basic_string_view<char>( 
 506       reinterpret_cast<const char*>(s.data()), s.size())); 
 507 } 
 508  
 509 template <typename Char> 
 510 inline size_t code_point_index(basic_string_view<Char> s, size_t n) { 
 511   size_t size = s.size(); 
 512   return n < size ? n : size; 
 513 } 
 514  
 515 // Calculates the index of the nth code point in a UTF-8 string. 
 516 inline size_t code_point_index(basic_string_view<char8_type> s, size_t n) { 
 517   const char8_type* data = s.data(); 
 518   size_t num_code_points = 0; 
 519   for (size_t i = 0, size = s.size(); i != size; ++i) { 
 520     if ((data[i] & 0xc0) != 0x80 && ++num_code_points > n) { 
 521       return i; 
 522     } 
 523   } 
 524   return s.size(); 
 525 } 
 526  
 527 template <typename InputIt, typename OutChar> 
 528 using needs_conversion = bool_constant< 
 529     std::is_same<typename std::iterator_traits<InputIt>::value_type, 
 530                  char>::value && 
 531     std::is_same<OutChar, char8_type>::value>; 
 532  
 533 template <typename OutChar, typename InputIt, typename OutputIt, 
 534           FMT_ENABLE_IF(!needs_conversion<InputIt, OutChar>::value)> 
 535 OutputIt copy_str(InputIt begin, InputIt end, OutputIt it) { 
 536   return std::copy(begin, end, it); 
 537 } 
 538  
 539 template <typename OutChar, typename InputIt, typename OutputIt, 
 540           FMT_ENABLE_IF(needs_conversion<InputIt, OutChar>::value)> 
 541 OutputIt copy_str(InputIt begin, InputIt end, OutputIt it) { 
 542   return std::transform(begin, end, it, 
 543                         [](char c) { return static_cast<char8_type>(c); }); 
 544 }

 546 #ifndef FMT_USE_GRISU 
 547 #  define FMT_USE_GRISU 1 
 548 #endif 
 549  
 550 template <typename T> constexpr bool use_grisu() { 
 551   return FMT_USE_GRISU && std::numeric_limits<double>::is_iec559 && 
 552          sizeof(T) <= sizeof(double); 
 553 } 
 554  
 555 template <typename T> 
 556 template <typename U> 
 557 void buffer<T>::append(const U* begin, const U* end) { 
 558   do { 
 559     auto count = to_unsigned(end - begin); 
 560     try_reserve(size_ + count); 
 561     auto free_cap = capacity_ - size_; 
 562     if (free_cap < count) count = free_cap; 
 563     std::uninitialized_copy_n(begin, count, make_checked(ptr_ + size_, count)); 
 564     size_ += count; 
 565     begin += count; 
 566   } while (begin != end); 
 567 } 
 568  
 569 template <typename OutputIt, typename T, typename Traits> 
 570 void iterator_buffer<OutputIt, T, Traits>::flush() { 
 571   out_ = std::copy_n(data_, this->limit(this->size()), out_); 
 572   this->clear(); 
 573 } 
 574 }  // namespace detail 
 575  
 576 // The number of characters to store in the basic_memory_buffer object itself 
 577 // to avoid dynamic memory allocation. 
 578 enum { inline_buffer_size = 500 }; 
 579  
 580 /** 
 581   \rst 
 582   A dynamically growing memory buffer for trivially copyable/constructible types 
 583   with the first ``SIZE`` elements stored in the object itself. 
 584  
 585   You can use one of the following type aliases for common character types: 
 586  
 587   +----------------+------------------------------+ 
 588   | Type           | Definition                   | 
 589   +================+==============================+ 
 590   | memory_buffer  | basic_memory_buffer<char>    | 
 591   +----------------+------------------------------+ 
 592   | wmemory_buffer | basic_memory_buffer<wchar_t> | 
 593   +----------------+------------------------------+ 
 594  
 595   **Example**:: 
 596  
 597      fmt::memory_buffer out; 
 598      format_to(out, "The answer is {}.", 42); 
 599  
 600   This will append the following output to the ``out`` object: 
 601  
 602   .. code-block:: none 
 603  
 604      The answer is 42. 
 605  
 606   The output can be converted to an ``std::string`` with ``to_string(out)``. 
 607   \endrst 
 608  */ 
 609 template <typename T, size_t SIZE = inline_buffer_size, 
 610           typename Allocator = std::allocator<T>> 
 611 class basic_memory_buffer : public detail::buffer<T> { 
 612  private: 
 613   T store_[SIZE]; 
 614  
 615   // Don't inherit from Allocator avoid generating type_info for it. 
 616   Allocator alloc_; 
 617  
 618   // Deallocate memory allocated by the buffer. 
 619   void deallocate() { 
 620     T* data = this->data(); 
 621     if (data != store_) alloc_.deallocate(data, this->capacity()); 
 622   } 
 623  
 624  protected: 
 625   void grow(size_t size) final; 
 626  
 627  public: 
 628   using value_type = T; 
 629   using const_reference = const T&; 
 630  
 631   explicit basic_memory_buffer(const Allocator& alloc = Allocator()) 
 632       : alloc_(alloc) { 
 633     this->set(store_, SIZE); 
 634   } 
 635   ~basic_memory_buffer() { deallocate(); } 
 636  
 637  private: 
 638   // Move data from other to this buffer. 
 639   void move(basic_memory_buffer& other) { 
 640     alloc_ = std::move(other.alloc_); 
 641     T* data = other.data(); 
 642     size_t size = other.size(), capacity = other.capacity(); 
 643     if (data == other.store_) { 
 644       this->set(store_, capacity); 
 645       std::uninitialized_copy(other.store_, other.store_ + size, 
 646                               detail::make_checked(store_, capacity)); 
 647     } else { 
 648       this->set(data, capacity); 
 649       // Set pointer to the inline array so that delete is not called 
 650       // when deallocating. 
 651       other.set(other.store_, 0); 
 652     } 
 653     this->resize(size); 
 654   } 
 655  
 656  public: 
 657   /** 
 658     \rst 
 659     Constructs a :class:`fmt::basic_memory_buffer` object moving the content 
 660     of the other object to it. 
 661     \endrst 
 662    */ 
 663   basic_memory_buffer(basic_memory_buffer&& other) FMT_NOEXCEPT { move(other); } 
 664  
 665   /** 
 666     \rst 
 667     Moves the content of the other ``basic_memory_buffer`` object to this one. 
 668     \endrst 
 669    */ 
 670   basic_memory_buffer& operator=(basic_memory_buffer&& other) FMT_NOEXCEPT { 
 671     FMT_ASSERT(this != &other, ""); 
 672     deallocate(); 
 673     move(other); 
 674     return *this; 
 675   } 
 676  
 677   // Returns a copy of the allocator associated with this buffer. 
 678   Allocator get_allocator() const { return alloc_; } 
 679  
 680   /** 
 681     Resizes the buffer to contain *count* elements. If T is a POD type new 
 682     elements may not be initialized. 
 683    */ 
 684   void resize(size_t count) { this->try_resize(count); } 
 685  
 686   /** Increases the buffer capacity to *new_capacity*. */ 
 687   void reserve(size_t new_capacity) { this->try_reserve(new_capacity); } 
 688 }; 
 689  
 690 template <typename T, size_t SIZE, typename Allocator> 
 691 void basic_memory_buffer<T, SIZE, Allocator>::grow(size_t size) { 
 692 #ifdef FMT_FUZZ 
 693   if (size > 5000) throw std::runtime_error("fuzz mode - won't grow that much"); 
 694 #endif 
 695   size_t old_capacity = this->capacity(); 
 696   size_t new_capacity = old_capacity + old_capacity / 2; 
 697   if (size > new_capacity) new_capacity = size; 
 698   T* old_data = this->data(); 
 699   T* new_data = 
 700       std::allocator_traits<Allocator>::allocate(alloc_, new_capacity); 
 701   // The following code doesn't throw, so the raw pointer above doesn't leak. 
 702   std::uninitialized_copy(old_data, old_data + this->size(), 
 703                           detail::make_checked(new_data, new_capacity)); 
 704   this->set(new_data, new_capacity); 
 705   // deallocate must not throw according to the standard, but even if it does, 
 706   // the buffer already uses the new storage and will deallocate it in 
 707   // destructor. 
 708   if (old_data != store_) alloc_.deallocate(old_data, old_capacity); 
 709 } 
 710  
 711 using memory_buffer = basic_memory_buffer<char>; 
 712 using wmemory_buffer = basic_memory_buffer<wchar_t>; 
 713  
 714 template <typename T, size_t SIZE, typename Allocator> 
 715 struct is_contiguous<basic_memory_buffer<T, SIZE, Allocator>> : std::true_type { 
 716 };

 739 // Returns true if value is negative, false otherwise. 
 740 // Same as `value < 0` but doesn't produce warnings if T is an unsigned type. 
 741 template <typename T, FMT_ENABLE_IF(is_signed<T>::value)> 
 742 FMT_CONSTEXPR bool is_negative(T value) { 
 743   return value < 0; 
 744 }

 746 FMT_CONSTEXPR bool is_negative(T) {

 753          (std::is_same<T, double>::value && FMT_USE_DOUBLE) ||

 757 #if FMT_REDUCE_INT_INSTANTIATIONS 
 758 // Pick the largest integer container to represent all values of T. 
 759 template <typename T> 
 760 using uint32_or_64_or_128_t = 
 761     conditional_t<sizeof(uint128_t) < sizeof(uint64_t), uint64_t, uint128_t>; 
 762 #else 
 763 // Smallest of uint32_t, uint64_t, uint128_t that is large enough to 
 764 // represent all values of T. 
 765 template <typename T> 
 766 using uint32_or_64_or_128_t = 
 767     conditional_t<num_bits<T>() <= 32, uint32_t, 
 768                   conditional_t<num_bits<T>() <= 64, uint64_t, uint128_t>>; 
 769 #endif

 771 // Static data is placed in this class template for the header-only config. 
 772 template <typename T = void> struct FMT_EXTERN_TEMPLATE_API basic_data { 
 773   // Maps the result of bsr(n) to ceil(log10(n)). 
 774   static const uint16_t bsr2log10[]; 
 775   static const uint64_t powers_of_10_64[]; 
 776   static const uint32_t zero_or_powers_of_10_32[]; 
 777   static const uint64_t zero_or_powers_of_10_64[]; 
 778   static const uint64_t pow10_significands[]; 
 779   static const int16_t pow10_exponents[]; 
 780   // GCC generates slightly better code for pairs than chars. 
 781   using digit_pair = char[2]; 
 782   static const digit_pair digits[]; 
 783   static const char hex_digits[]; 
 784   static const char foreground_color[]; 
 785   static const char background_color[]; 
 786   static const char reset_color[5]; 
 787   static const wchar_t wreset_color[5]; 
 788   static const char signs[]; 
 789   static const char left_padding_shifts[5]; 
 790   static const char right_padding_shifts[5]; 
 791 };

 797 // This is a struct rather than an alias to avoid shadowing warnings in gcc.

 800 #ifdef FMT_BUILTIN_CLZLL 
 801 // Returns the number of decimal digits in n. Leading zeros are not counted 
 802 // except for n == 0 in which case count_digits returns 1. 
 803 inline int count_digits(uint64_t n) { 
 804   // https://github.com/fmtlib/format-benchmark/blob/master/digits10 
 805   auto t = data::bsr2log10[FMT_BUILTIN_CLZLL(n | 1) ^ 63]; 
 806   return t - (n < data::zero_or_powers_of_10_64[t - 1]); 
 807 } 
 808 #else 
 809 // Fallback version of count_digits used when __builtin_clz is not available. 
 810 inline int count_digits(uint64_t n) { 
 811   int count = 1; 
 812   for (;;) { 
 813     // Integer division is slow so do it for a group of four digits instead 
 814     // of for every digit. The idea comes from the talk by Alexandrescu 
 815     // "Three Optimization Tips for C++". See speed-test for a comparison. 
 816     if (n < 10) return count; 
 817     if (n < 100) return count + 1; 
 818     if (n < 1000) return count + 2; 
 819     if (n < 10000) return count + 3; 
 820     n /= 10000u; 
 821     count += 4; 
 822   } 
 823 } 
 824 #endif 
 825  
 826 #if FMT_USE_INT128 
 827 inline int count_digits(uint128_t n) { 
 828   int count = 1; 
 829   for (;;) { 
 830     // Integer division is slow so do it for a group of four digits instead 
 831     // of for every digit. The idea comes from the talk by Alexandrescu 
 832     // "Three Optimization Tips for C++". See speed-test for a comparison. 
 833     if (n < 10) return count; 
 834     if (n < 100) return count + 1; 
 835     if (n < 1000) return count + 2; 
 836     if (n < 10000) return count + 3; 
 837     n /= 10000U; 
 838     count += 4; 
 839   } 
 840 } 
 841 #endif 
 842  
 843 // Counts the number of digits in n. BITS = log2(radix). 
 844 template <unsigned BITS, typename UInt> inline int count_digits(UInt n) { 
 845   int num_digits = 0; 
 846   do { 
 847     ++num_digits; 
 848   } while ((n >>= BITS) != 0); 
 849   return num_digits; 
 850 } 
 851  
 852 template <> int count_digits<4>(detail::fallback_uintptr n); 
 853  
 854 #if FMT_GCC_VERSION || FMT_CLANG_VERSION 
 855 #  define FMT_ALWAYS_INLINE inline __attribute__((always_inline)) 
 856 #else 
 857 #  define FMT_ALWAYS_INLINE 
 858 #endif 
 859  
 860 #ifdef FMT_BUILTIN_CLZ 
 861 // Optional version of count_digits for better performance on 32-bit platforms. 
 862 inline int count_digits(uint32_t n) { 
 863   auto t = data::bsr2log10[FMT_BUILTIN_CLZ(n | 1) ^ 31]; 
 864   return t - (n < data::zero_or_powers_of_10_32[t - 1]); 
 865 } 
 866 #endif

 870 } 
 871 template <> constexpr int digits10<int128_t>() FMT_NOEXCEPT { return 38; } 
 872 template <> constexpr int digits10<uint128_t>() FMT_NOEXCEPT { return 38; }
